TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example: execute_command(command="ls -l") — omit blocking as it defaults to true.\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example: execute_command(command="npm run dev", blocking="false", session_name="dev")\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use -name for filename patterns\n    - Use -type for file types\n  5. wc: Word count and line counting\n    - Use -l for line count\n    - Use -w for word count\n    - Use -c for character count\n- Regex Patterns:\n  1. Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. COMMUNICATION & USER INTERACTION\n\n## 7.1 CONVERSATIONAL INTERACTIONS\nFor casual conversation and social interactions:\n  - ALWAYS use **\'ask\'** tool to end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - NEVER use \'complete\' for casual conversation\n  - Keep responses friendly and natural\n  - Adapt to user\'s communication style\n  - Ask follow-up questions when appropriate (**using \'ask\'**)\n  - Show interest in user\'s responses\n\n## 7.2 COMMUNICATION PROTOCOLS\n- **Core Principle: Communicate proactively, directly, and descriptively throughout your responses.**\n\n- **Narrative-Style Communication:**\n  * Integrate descriptive Markdown-formatted text directly in your responses before, between, and after tool calls\n  * Use a conversational yet efficient tone that conveys what you\'re doing and why\n  * Structure your communication with Markdown headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting for enhanced readability\n  * Balance detail with conciseness - be informative without being verbose\n\n- **Communication Structure:**\n  * Begin tasks with a brief overview of your plan\n  * Provide context headers like `## Planning`, `### Researching`, `## Creating File`, etc.\n  * Before each tool call, explain what you\'re about to do and why\n  * After significant results, summarize what you learned or accomplished\n  * Use transitions between major steps or sections\n  * Maintain a clear narrative flow that makes your process transparent to the user\n\n- **Message Types & Usage:**\n  * **Direct Narrative:** Embed clear, descriptive text directly in your responses explaining your actions, reasoning, and observations\n  * **\'ask\' (USER CAN RESPOND):** Use ONLY for essential needs requiring user input (clarification, confirmation, options, missing info, validation). Built with a modern microservices architecture featuring FastAPI, Next.js, OpenCV, and YOLOv8.\\n</p>\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📋 Table of Contents\\n\\n- [Overview](#overview)\\n- [Architecture](#architecture)\\n- [Features](#features)\\n- [Tech Stack](#tech-stack)\\n- [Project Structure](#project-structure)\\n- [Quick Start](#quick-start)\\n- [Environment Configuration](#environment-configuration)\\n- [API Reference](#api-reference)\\n- [AI Engine](#ai-engine)\\n- [Deployment](#deployment)\\n- [Development](#development)\\n- [Testing](#testing)\\n- [Contributing](#contributing)\\n- [License](#license)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🌟 Overview\\n\\nThe Vehicle Detection & Speed Tracking Platform is an enterprise-grade solution designed for traffic monitoring, law enforcement, and smart city infrastructure. It combines state-of-the-art computer vision algorithms with a modern web application stack to deliver real-time vehicle detection, multi-object tracking, and speed estimation capabilities through an intuitive dashboard interface.\\n\\nThe platform processes video from uploaded files or live RTSP/CCTV streams, identifies vehicles using both Haar Cascade classifiers and YOLOv8 deep learning models, tracks each vehicle with unique identifiers using the dlib correlation tracker, and calculates instantaneous and average speeds with configurable pixels-per-meter calibration. All results are displayed through a glassmorphism-themed dashboard with real-time WebSocket updates, interactive charts, and downloadable analytics reports.\\n\\n### Key Capabilities\\n\\nThe system supports dual detection backends with automatic fallback — YOLOv8 for high-accuracy deep learning inference and Haar Cascade for lightweight, CPU-friendly processing. Vehicle tracking uses dlib\'s correlation tracker to maintain persistent identities across frames, even through partial occlusions and brief disappearances. Speed estimation employs a multi-frame smoothing algorithm with configurable pixels-per-meter calibration to convert pixel displacements into real-world speed measurements in kilometers per hour. The lane detection module uses Canny edge detection and Hough line transforms to identify road lanes and assign vehicles to specific lanes for per-lane analytics.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🏗️ Architecture\\n\\n```\\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n│                        Nginx (Reverse Proxy)                │\\n│                   SSL Termination / WebSocket               │\\n├────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│                        │                                    │\\n│   ┌────────────────┐   │   ┌────────────────────────────┐   │\\n│   │   Frontend     │   │   │        Backend             │   │\\n│   │   Next.js 14   │◄──┼──►│        FastAPI             │   │\\n│   │   React 18     │   │   │        Python 3.11         │   │\\n│   │   TypeScript   │   │   │                             │   │\\n│   │   Tailwind CSS │   │   │   ┌─────────────────────┐  │   │\\n│   │   ShadCN UI    │   │   │   │    AI Engine        │  │   │\\n│   └────────────────┘   │   │   │    OpenCV + YOLOv8  │  │   │\\n│                        │   │   │    dlib Tracker      │  │   │\\n│                        │   │   │    Speed Estimator   │  │   │\\n│                        │   │   └─────────────────────┘  │   │\\n│                        │   │            │                │   │\\n│                        │   │   ┌────────┴─────────┐     │   │\\n│                        │   │   │  WebSocket Mgr   │     │   │\\n│                        │   │   │  Background Tasks│     │   │\\n│                        │   │   └──────────────────┘     │   │\\n│                        │   └────────────────────────────┘   │\\n│                        │            │           │           │\\n│                   ┌────┴────┐  ┌────┴───┐  ┌────┴────┐    │\\n│                   │PostgreSQL│  │ Redis  │  │FileSystem│   │\\n│                   └─────────┘  └────────┘  └─────────┘    │\\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\\n```\\n\\n### Data Flow\\n\\n1. User uploads a video or connects an RTSP stream via the web interface\\n2. The backend receives the input and enqueues a processing job via Redis\\n3. The AI Engine pipeline processes each frame: detection → tracking → speed estimation → lane assignment\\n4. Results are stored in PostgreSQL and broadcast via WebSocket to connected clients\\n5. The frontend dashboard updates in real-time with bounding boxes, speed readings, and alerts\\n6. Analytics data is aggregated for charts, heatmaps, and downloadable reports\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ✨ Features\\n\\n### Vehicle Detection\\n- **Dual Detection Backend**: YOLOv8 (primary) with Haar Cascade fallback for CPU-constrained environments\\n- **Multi-Class Detection**: Cars, trucks, buses, motorcycles, bicycles, and vans\\n- **Confidence Filtering**: Configurable confidence threshold to reduce false positives\\n- **Bounding Box Visualization**: Real-time overlay with color-coded vehicle types\\n\\n### Vehicle Tracking\\n- **Persistent Identity**: dlib correlation tracker assigns unique IDs that persist across frames\\n- **Occlusion Handling**: Tracks survive brief occlusions with configurable missing-frame tolerance\\n- **Position Trail**: Visual trail of previous positions for trajectory analysis\\n- **Lost Vehicle Management**: Automatic cleanup of tracks that exceed the missing-frame threshold\\n\\n### Speed Estimation\\n- **Real-Time Calculation**: Speed computed from pixel displacement, FPS, and pixels-per-meter calibration\\n- **Smoothing Algorithm**: Moving average filter to reduce measurement noise\\n- **Overspeed Detection**: Configurable speed limit with instant flagging of violations\\n- **Multi-Frame Averaging**: Aggregates speed readings across multiple frames for stability\\n\\n### Lane Detection\\n- **Automatic Lane Finding**: Canny edge detection combined with Hough line transforms\\n- **Region of Interest**: Configurable ROI masking to focus on the road surface\\n- **Vehicle-to-Lane Assignment**: Each detected vehicle is assigned to the nearest lane\\n- **Per-Lane Statistics**: Speed and traffic volume broken down by lane\\n\\n### Dashboard & Analytics\\n- **Real-Time Monitoring**: Live video feed with overlaid detections and speed readings\\n- **Interactive Charts**: Speed trends, vehicle type distribution, hourly traffic patterns\\n- **Detection Heatmap**: Visual density map of detection hotspots by day and hour\\n- **Overspeed Alerts**: Real-time notifications for speed limit violations\\n- **Downloadable Reports**: PDF and CSV exports of detection data and analytics\\n\\n### System Features\\n- **JWT Authentication**: Secure token-based auth with role-based access control (admin, operator, viewer)\\n- **WebSocket Updates**: Real-time push notifications for detections, alerts, and analytics\\n- **Dark/Light Theme**: Glassmorphism UI with smooth theme transitions\\n- **Responsive Design**: Fully responsive layout optimized for desktop and tablet\\n- **Multi-Language Ready**: Internationalization infrastructure in place\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🛠️ Tech Stack\\n\\n### Frontend\\n| Technology | Version | Purpose |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Next.js | 14+ | React framework with App Router ├── speed_estimator.py       # Speed calculation engine\\n│   ├── lane_detector.py         # Lane detection module\\n│   └── pipeline.py              # Processing pipeline orchestrator\\n│\\n├──                     # Utility scripts\\n│   ├── setup.sh                 # Initial project setup\\n│   ├── seed_db.py               # Database seeding\\n│   ├── run_dev.sh               # Development environment launcher\\n│   └── backup_db.sh             # Database backup utility\\n│\\n├── docs/                        # Documentation\\n│   ├── api.md                   # API reference docker-compose.yml           # Production Docker Compose\\n├── .github/workflows/\\n│   └── ci-cd.yml                # GitHub Actions CI/CD\\n└── README.md                    # This file\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🚀 Quick Start\\n\\n### Prerequisites\\n\\n- **Docker** 24.0+ and Docker Compose 2.20+\\n- **Node.js** 20+ and npm 10+ (for local frontend development)\\n- **Python** 3.11+ and pip 23+ (for local backend development)\\n- **PostgreSQL** 16+ (if running without Docker)\\n- **Redis** 7+ (if running without Docker)\\n\\n### Option 1: Docker Compose (Recommended)\\n\\nThe fastest way to get the entire platform running is with Docker Compose. This spins up all services — PostgreSQL, Redis, the FastAPI backend, the Next.js frontend, and the Nginx reverse proxy — with a single command.\\n\\n```bash\\n# Clone the repository\\ngit clone https://github.com/your-org/vehicle-speed-tracking.git\\ncd vehicle-speed-tracking\\n\\n# Copy environment variables\\ncp backend/.env.example backend/.env\\n\\n# Edit the environment file with your configuration\\n# At minimum, set a secure JWT_SECRET and database credentials\\nnano backend/.env\\n\\n# Start all services\\ndocker compose up -d\\n\\n# Run database migrations (first time only)\\ndocker compose exec backend python -c \\"from app.db.session import init_db; import asyncio; asyncio.run(init_db())\\"\\n\\n# Seed the database with sample data (optional)\\ndocker compose exec backend python scripts/seed_db.py\\n```\\n\\nThe platform will be available at:\\n- **Frontend**: http://localhost (port 80 via Nginx)\\n- **Backend API**: http://localhost/api/v1\\n- **API Docs (Swagger)**: http://localhost/api/docs\\n- **API Docs (ReDoc)**: http://localhost/api/redoc\\n\\n### Option 2: Local Development\\n\\nFor active development, you may want to run the frontend and backend separately for hot-reload support.\\n\\n#### Backend Setup\\n\\n```bash\\ncd backend\\n\\n# Create a virtual environment\\npython -m venv venv\\nsource venv/bin/activate  # On Windows: venv\\\\Scripts\\\\activate\\n\\n# Install dependencies\\npip install -r requirements.txt\\n\\n# Copy and configure environment variables\\ncp .env.example .env\\n# Edit .env with your PostgreSQL and Redis connection details\\n\\n# Start PostgreSQL and Redis (if not using Docker)\\n# Ensure they\'re running on the configured ports\\n\\n# Run database migrations\\npython -c \\"from app.db.session import init_db; import asyncio; asyncio.run(init_db())\\"\\n\\n# Start the backend server with auto-reload\\nuvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload\\n```\\n\\n#### Frontend Setup\\n\\n```bash\\ncd frontend\\n\\n# Install dependencies\\nnpm install\\n\\n# Configure the API base URL (defaults to http://localhost:8000)\\n# Create .env.local if you need to override:\\necho \\"NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:8000\\" > .env.local\\n\\n# Start the development server\\nnpm run dev\\n```\\n\\nThe frontend will be available at http://localhost:3000 and the backend at http://localhost:8000.\\n\\n### Default Login Credentials\\n\\nAfter seeding the database, you can log in with these default accounts:\\n\\n| Role | Email | Password |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Admin | admin@vstrack.io | admin123 |\\n| Operator | operator@vstrack.io | operator123 |\\n| Viewer | viewer@vstrack.io | viewer123 |\\n\\n> ⚠️ **Security Warning**: Change these default passwords immediately in production. Use the settings page or the API to update user credentials.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ⚙️ Environment Configuration\\n\\nAll configuration is managed through environment variables. The backend uses Pydantic Settings for validated configuration with sensible defaults.\\n\\n### Backend Environment Variables\\n\\nCreate a `.env` file in the `backend/` directory (see `.env.example` for the full template):\\n\\n```env\\n# Application\\nAPP_NAME=\\"VSTrack - Vehicle Speed Tracking\\"\\nAPP_VERSION=1.0.0\\nDEBUG=false\\nAPI_PREFIX=/api/v1\\n\\n# Server\\nHOST=0.0.0.0\\nPORT=8000\\n\\n# Database\\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://vstrack:vstrack123@localhost:5432/vstrack_db\\n\\n# Redis\\nREDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0\\n\\n# JWT Authentication\\nJWT_SECRET=your-super-secret-jwt-key-change-in-production\\nJWT_ALGORITHM=HS256\\nACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES=30\\nREFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRE_DAYS=7\\n\\n# CORS\\nCORS_ORIGINS=[\\"http://localhost:3000\\",\\"http://localhost\\"]\\n\\n# Detection Configuration\\nDETECTION_MODEL=yolov8n\\nDETECTION_CONFIDENCE=0.5\\nTRACKER_MAX_FRAMES_MISSING=30\\nSPEED_SMOOTHING_WINDOW=5\\n\\n# Speed Estimation\\nDEFAULT_PIXELS_PER_METER=10.0\\nDEFAULT_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH=60.0\\nMIN_SPEED_KMH=5.0\\n\\n# File Storage\\nUPLOAD_DIR=./uploads\\nMAX_UPLOAD_SIZE_MB=500\\n```\\n\\n### Frontend Environment Variables\\n\\nCreate a `.env.local` file in the `frontend/` directory:\\n\\n```env\\nNEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:8000\\nNEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL=ws://localhost:8000\\n```\\n\\n### Key Configuration Details\\n\\n**Detection Model**: Set `DETECTION_MODEL` to `yolov8n` (nano), `yolov8s` (small), `yolov8m` (medium), `yolov8l` (large), or `yolov8x` (extra-large). The nano model is fastest; the extra-large model is most accurate. If YOLOv8 is unavailable, the system automatically falls back to Haar Cascade detection.\\n\\n**Pixels Per Meter (PPM)**: This is the critical calibration value for speed estimation. It represents how many pixels in the video frame correspond to one real-world meter. This value varies based on camera height, angle, and resolution. You can configure a global default and override it per-camera through the camera management interface. To calibrate, measure a known distance in the real scene and count the corresponding pixels in the video frame.\\n\\n**Speed Limit**: The `DEFAULT_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH` sets the threshold for overspeed detection. Vehicles exceeding this speed are flagged in the dashboard and trigger WebSocket alerts. This can also be overridden per-camera.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📡 API Reference\\n\\nThe backend exposes a RESTful API under `/api/v1/` with full Swagger documentation available at `/api/docs` when the server is running. Below is a summary of the available endpoints.\\n\\n### Authentication\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| POST | `/api/v1/auth/register` | Register a new user account |\\n| POST | `/api/v1/auth/login` | Authenticate and receive JWT tokens |\\n| POST | `/api/v1/auth/refresh` | Refresh an expired access token |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/auth/me` | Get the current authenticated user |\\n\\n### Vehicles\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| GET | `/api/v1/vehicles/` | List vehicles with filtering and pagination |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/vehicles/{id}` | Get a specific vehicle\'s details |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/vehicles/{id}/speed-logs` | Get speed history for a vehicle |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/vehicles/stats/summary` | Get vehicle statistics summary |\\n\\n### Cameras\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| GET | `/api/v1/cameras/` | List all cameras |\\n| POST | `/api/v1/cameras/` | Register a new camera (admin only) |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/cameras/{id}` | Get camera details |\\n| PUT | `/api/v1/cameras/{id}` | Update camera configuration (admin only) |\\n| DELETE | `/api/v1/cameras/{id}` | Remove a camera (admin only) |\\n\\n### Video Processing\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| POST | `/api/v1/video/upload` | Upload a video file for processing |\\n| POST | `/api/v1/video/stream` | Start processing an RTSP stream |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/video/status/{job_id}` | Check processing job status |\\n\\n### Analytics\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/dashboard` | Get dashboard summary statistics |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/speed-trend` | Get speed trend over time |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/hourly-distribution` | Get hourly traffic distribution |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/overspeed-events` | Get overspeed violation events |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/vehicle-type-stats` | Get vehicle type distribution |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/analytics/heatmap` | Get detection heatmap data |\\n\\n### Reports\\n\\n| Method | Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| GET | `/api/v1/reports/csv` | Download detection data as CSV |\\n| GET | `/api/v1/reports/pdf` | Download analytics report as PDF |\\n\\n### WebSocket\\n\\n| Endpoint | Description |\\n|---|---|\\n| `ws://host/api/v1/ws/{channel}` | Real-time updates (channels: `detections`, `alerts`, `analytics`) |\\n\\nFor complete request/response schemas and interactive testing, visit the Swagger UI at `/api/docs`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🤖 AI Engine\\n\\nThe AI Engine is the core processing module that handles all computer vision tasks. It is designed as a standalone Python package that can be imported by the FastAPI backend or used independently for batch processing.\\n\\n### Detection Module (`detector.py`)\\n\\nThe detection module provides two complementary detection strategies:\\n\\n**HaarCascadeDetector** uses OpenCV\'s pre-trained cascade classifiers for vehicle detection. It is lightweight, requires no GPU, and works well for frontal and rear views of vehicles. The classifier files are bundled with OpenCV, so no additional model downloads are needed. It is best suited for low-resource environments or as a fallback when YOLOv8 is unavailable.\\n\\n**YOLOv8Detector** uses the Ultralytics YOLOv8 model for state-of-the-art object detection. It filters results to vehicle-relevant COCO classes: car (2), motorcycle (3), bus (5), and truck (7). The model is automatically downloaded on first use. YOLOv8 provides significantly higher accuracy and better handles varied viewing angles, partial occlusions, and diverse vehicle types. It is the recommended detector for production deployments.\\n\\n**VehicleDetector** is the unified interface that attempts YOLOv8 detection first and falls back to Haar Cascade if the YOLOv8 model fails to load or encounters an error. This ensures the system remains operational even if deep learning dependencies are unavailable.\\n\\n### Tracking Module (`tracker.py`)\\n\\nThe tracking module uses dlib\'s correlation tracker for multi-object tracking. Each new detection is either matched to an existing tracker (if the bounding box overlap exceeds the IoU threshold) or assigned a new unique tracking ID. The tracker maintains a history of positions for each vehicle, enabling trail visualization and displacement calculation.\\n\\nKey parameters include `max_frames_missing` (default: 30), which determines how many frames a tracker can survive without a matching detection before being removed, and the IoU threshold for matching detections to existing trackers.\\n\\n### Speed Estimation Module (`speed_estimator.py`)\\n\\nSpeed estimation converts pixel displacements into real-world speed measurements using the formula: **speed (km/h) = d_meters × fps × 3.6**, where `d_meters = pixel_distance / pixels_per_meter`. The module applies a moving average smoothing filter (configurable window size) to reduce noise from frame-to-frame jitter. It also enforces a minimum displacement threshold to avoid spurious speed readings when a vehicle is nearly stationary.\\n\\nThe speed estimator maintains per-vehicle statistics including current speed, average speed, maximum speed, and an overspeed flag that triggers when the configured speed limit is exceeded.\\n\\n### Lane Detection Module (`lane_detector.py`)\\n\\nLane detection uses a classical computer vision pipeline: the frame is converted to grayscale, a region of interest mask is applied to focus on the road surface, Canny edge detection identifies edges, and Hough line transforms extract straight lines corresponding to lane markings. Detected lines are clustered and averaged to produce lane boundaries. Each tracked vehicle is then assigned to the nearest lane based on its centroid position.\\n\\n### Processing Pipeline (`pipeline.py`)\\n\\nThe pipeline orchestrates the full processing workflow. It accepts a `PipelineConfig` with all configurable parameters and coordinates the detector, tracker, speed estimator, and lane detector. It provides both synchronous and asynchronous processing methods for video files and live streams. Callback hooks (`on_frame`, `on_overspeed`) enable real-time integration with the WebSocket system for pushing updates to connected clients.\\n\\nThe pipeline also generates a HUD overlay on processed frames showing the current frame number, vehicle count, average speed, and any overspeed alerts — useful for monitoring and debugging.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🐳 Deployment\\n\\nFor comprehensive deployment instructions including Docker production setup, SSL configuration, scaling strategies, and monitoring, see [docs/deployment.md](docs/deployment.md).\\n\\nFor local environment setup and development configuration, see [docs/environment-setup.md](docs/environment-setup.md).\\n\\n### Quick Production Deployment\\n\\n```bash\\n# Build and start all services\\ndocker compose -f docker-compose.yml up -d --build\\n\\n# Verify all services are running\\ndocker compose ps\\n\\n# Check logs\\ndocker compose logs -f backend\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 💻 Development\\n\\n### Running in Development Mode\\n\\n```bash\\n# Use the development Docker Compose configuration\\ndocker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d\\n\\n# Or run services individually with hot-reload:\\n# Terminal 1 - Backend\\ncd backend && uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000\\n\\n# Terminal 2 - Frontend\\ncd frontend && npm run dev\\n```\\n\\n### Code Style\\n\\n- **Frontend**: ESLint + Prettier (configured via `eslint.config.mjs`)\\n- **Backend**: Black formatter + isort + flake8\\n- **Type Safety**: Strict TypeScript mode enabled\\n\\n### Database Migrations\\n\\n```bash\\n# Create a new migration after model changes\\ncd backend\\nalembic revision --autogenerate -m \\"description of change\\"\\n\\n# Apply pending migrations\\nalembic upgrade head\\n\\n# Rollback the last migration\\nalembic downgrade -1\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧪 Testing\\n\\n### Backend Tests\\n\\n```bash\\ncd backend\\npip install pytest pytest-asyncio httpx\\n\\n# Run all tests\\npytest\\n\\n# Run with coverage\\npytest --cov=app --cov-report=html\\n```\\n\\n### Frontend Tests\\n\\n```bash\\ncd frontend\\n\\n# Run unit tests\\nnpm test\\n\\n# Run e2e tests\\nnpx playwright test\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🤝 Contributing\\n\\nWe welcome contributions to the Vehicle Detection & Speed Tracking Platform. To contribute:\\n\\n1. Fork the repository\\n2. Create a feature branch (`git checkout -b feature/amazing-feature`)\\n3. Commit your changes (`git commit -m \'Add amazing feature\'`)\\n4. '{"file_contents": "# Deployment Guide\\n\\nThis guide covers deploying the Vehicle Detection & Speed Tracking Platform to production environments using Docker, Docker Compose, and Nginx.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Table of Contents\\n\\n- [Prerequisites](#prerequisites)\\n- [Docker Production Deployment](#docker-production-deployment)\\n- [SSL/TLS Configuration](#ssltls-configuration)\\n- [Environment Configuration](#environment-configuration)\\n- [Database Setup](#database-setup)\\n- [Redis Configuration](#redis-configuration)\\n- [Nginx Configuration](#nginx-configuration)\\n- [Scaling Strategy](#scaling-strategy)\\n- [Monitoring & Logging](#monitoring--logging)\\n- [Backup & Recovery](#backup--recovery)\\n- [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Prerequisites\\n\\n### System Requirements\\n\\n| Component | Minimum | Recommended |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| CPU | 2 cores | 8 cores (for YOLOv8 inference) |\\n| RAM | 4 GB | 16 GB |\\n| Storage | 20 GB | 100 GB SSD (for video storage) |\\n| GPU | None | NVIDIA GPU with CUDA (for YOLOv8 acceleration) |\\n\\n### Software Requirements\\n\\n- Docker Engine 24.0+\\n- Docker Compose 2.20+\\n- Git 2.40+\\n- OpenSSL (for SSL certificate generation)\\n\\n### Network Requirements\\n\\n- Port 80 (HTTP) and 443 (HTTPS) exposed for web access\\n- Outbound internet access for Docker image pulls\\n- RTSP stream accessibility if processing live camera feeds\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Docker Production Deployment\\n\\n### Step 1: Prepare the Server\\n\\n```bash\\n# Update system packages\\nsudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade -y\\n\\n# Install Docker\\ncurl -fsSL https://get.docker.com | sh\\nsudo usermod -aG docker $USER\\n\\n# Install Docker Compose\\nsudo apt install docker-compose-plugin\\n\\n# Verify installations\\ndocker --version\\ndocker compose version\\n```\\n\\n### Step 2: Clone and Configure\\n\\n```bash\\n# Clone the repository\\ngit clone https://github.com/your-org/vehicle-speed-tracking.git\\ncd vehicle-speed-tracking\\n\\n# Copy the environment template\\ncp backend/.env.example backend/.env\\n\\n# Generate a secure JWT secret\\nopenssl rand -hex 32\\n# Paste the output as JWT_SECRET in backend/.env\\n\\n# Edit the environment file\\nnano backend/.env\\n```\\n\\n### Step 3: Configure Production Environment\\n\\nEdit `backend/.env` with production values:\\n\\n```env\\n# Application\\nAPP_NAME=\\"VSTrack - Vehicle Speed Tracking\\"\\nAPP_VERSION=1.0.0\\nDEBUG=false\\nAPI_PREFIX=/api/v1\\n\\n# Server\\nHOST=0.0.0.0\\nPORT=8000\\n\\n# Database - Use strong passwords in production\\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://vstrack:STRONG_DB_PASSWORD@db:5432/vstrack_db\\n\\n# Redis\\nREDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379/0\\n\\n# JWT - Use the generated secret\\nJWT_SECRET=your-generated-secret-here\\nJWT_ALGORITHM=HS256\\nACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES=30\\nREFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRE_DAYS=7\\n\\n# CORS - Restrict to your domain\\nCORS_ORIGINS=[\\"https://your-domain.com\\"]\\n\\n# Detection\\nDETECTION_MODEL=yolov8n\\nDETECTION_CONFIDENCE=0.5\\nTRACKER_MAX_FRAMES_MISSING=30\\nSPEED_SMOOTHING_WINDOW=5\\n\\n# Speed Estimation\\nDEFAULT_PIXELS_PER_METER=10.0\\nDEFAULT_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH=60.0\\nMIN_SPEED_KMH=5.0\\n\\n# File Storage\\nUPLOAD_DIR=./uploads\\nMAX_UPLOAD_SIZE_MB=500\\n```\\n\\n### Step 4: Build and Deploy\\n\\n```bash\\n# Build all Docker images\\ndocker compose build\\n\\n# Start all services in detached mode\\ndocker compose up -d\\n\\n# Verify all containers are running\\ndocker compose ps\\n\\n# Expected output:\\n# NAME                STATUS          PORTS\\n# vstrack-db          Up 2 minutes    5432/tcp\\n# vstrack-redis       Up 2 minutes    6379/tcp\\n# vstrack-backend     Up 2 minutes    8000/tcp\\n# vstrack-frontend    Up 2 minutes    3000/tcp\\n# vstrack-nginx       Up 2 minutes    0.0.0.0:80->80/tcp\\n\\n# Initialize the database\\ndocker compose exec backend python -c \\"from app.db.session import init_db; import asyncio; asyncio.run(init_db())\\"\\n\\n# Seed with default admin user (optional)\\ndocker compose exec backend python scripts/seed_db.py\\n```\\n\\n### Step 5: Verify Deployment\\n\\n```bash\\n# Check backend health\\ncurl http://localhost/api/v1/health\\n\\n# Expected response:\\n# {\\"status\\": \\"healthy\\", \\"version\\": \\"1.0.0\\"}\\n\\n# Check the frontend\\ncurl -I http://localhost\\n\\n# Expected: HTTP/1.1 200 OK\\n\\n# View logs\\ndocker compose logs -f backend\\ndocker compose logs -f frontend\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## SSL/TLS Configuration\\n\\n### Using Let\'s Encrypt (Recommended)\\n\\n```bash\\n# Install Certbot\\nsudo apt install certbot python3-certbot-nginx -y\\n\\n# Obtain SSL certificate\\nsudo certbot --nginx -d your-domain.com -d www.your-domain.com\\n\\n# Certbot will automatically modify the Nginx configuration\\n# Verify auto-renewal is configured\\nsudo certbot renew --dry-run\\n```\\n\\n### Manual SSL Configuration\\n\\nEdit `nginx/nginx.conf` to enable HTTPS:\\n\\n```nginx\\nserver {\\n    listen 443 ssl http2;\\n    server_name your-domain.com;\\n\\n    ssl_certificate /etc/letsencrypt/live/your-domain.com/fullchain.pem;\\n    ssl_certificate_key /etc/letsencrypt/live/your-domain.com/privkey.pem;\\n    ssl_protocols TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3;\\n    ssl_ciphers HIGH:!aNULL:!MD5;\\n    ssl_prefer_server_ciphers on;\\n\\n    # ... rest of the location blocks\\n}\\n\\nserver {\\n    listen 80;\\n    server_name your-domain.com;\\n    return 301 https://$server_name$request_uri;\\n}\\n```\\n\\nThen update `docker-compose.yml` to mount the certificates:\\n\\n```yaml\\nnginx:\\n  volumes:\\n    - /etc/letsencrypt:/etc/letsencrypt:ro\\n  ports:\\n    - \\"80:80\\"\\n    - \\"443:443\\"\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Environment Configuration\\n\\n### Production Environment Checklist\\n\\nBefore deploying to production, ensure the following:\\n\\n1. **DEBUG=false** — Disable debug mode to prevent sensitive information leaks\\n2. **Strong JWT_SECRET** — Use a cryptographically random secret (at least 32 bytes)\\n3. **Strong database password** — Use a password with at least 16 characters\\n4. **CORS restricted** — Only allow your production domain origins\\n5. **Upload size limits** — Configure appropriate `MAX_UPLOAD_SIZE_MB`\\n6. **Rate limiting** — The built-in rate limiter is enabled by default\\n7. **HTTPS enforced** — All traffic should go through SSL/TLS\\n\\n### GPU Acceleration (Optional)\\n\\nIf you have an NVIDIA GPU available, you can accelerate YOLOv8 inference:\\n\\n1. Install the NVIDIA Container Toolkit on the host:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Add NVIDIA package repository\\ncurl -fsSL https://nvidia.github.io/libnvidia-container/gpgkey | sudo gpg --dearmor -o /usr/share/keyrings/nvidia-container-toolkit-keyring.gpg\\ncurl -s -L https://nvidia.github.io/libnvidia-container/stable/deb/nvidia-container-toolkit.list | \\\\\\n  sed \'s#deb https://#deb [signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/nvidia-container-toolkit-keyring.gpg] https://#g\' | \\\\\\n  sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/nvidia-container-toolkit.list\\n\\nsudo apt update\\nsudo apt install -y nvidia-container-toolkit\\nsudo systemctl restart docker\\n```\\n\\n2. Update `docker-compose.yml` to enable GPU access:\\n\\n```yaml\\nbackend:\\n  deploy:\\n    resources:\\n      reservations:\\n        devices:\\n          - driver: nvidia\\n            count: 1\\n            capabilities: [gpu]\\n```\\n\\n3. Install the GPU-enabled PyTorch in the backend Dockerfile:\\n\\n```dockerfile\\nRUN pip install torch torchvision --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Database Setup\\n\\n### PostgreSQL Configuration\\n\\nFor production, tune the PostgreSQL configuration for performance:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Connect to the PostgreSQL container\\ndocker compose exec db psql -U vstrack -d vstrack_db\\n\\n# Recommended settings for a server with 8GB RAM:\\n# Edit postgresql.conf via environment variables in docker-compose.yml:\\n```\\n\\nUpdate the `db` service in `docker-compose.yml`:\\n\\n```yaml\\ndb:\\n  image: postgres:16-alpine\\n  environment:\\n    POSTGRES_DB: vstrack_db\\n    POSTGRES_USER: vstrack\\n    POSTGRES_PASSWORD: STRONG_DB_PASSWORD\\n  command:\\n    - \\"postgres\\"\\n    - \\"-c\\"\\n    - \\"shared_buffers=2GB\\"\\n    - \\"-c\\"\\n    - \\"effective_cache_size=6GB\\"\\n    - \\"-c\\"\\n    - \\"work_mem=50MB\\"\\n    - \\"-c\\"\\n    - \\"maintenance_work_mem=512MB\\"\\n    - \\"-c\\"\\n    - \\"max_connections=100\\"\\n  volumes:\\n    - postgres_data:/var/lib/postgresql/data\\n```\\n\\n### Database Migrations\\n\\nWhen deploying updates that include model changes:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Generate a migration\\ndocker compose exec backend alembic revision --autogenerate -m \\"description\\"\\n\\n# Apply migrations\\ndocker compose exec backend alembic upgrade head\\n\\n# Rollback if needed\\ndocker compose exec backend alembic downgrade -1\\n```\\n\\n### Database Backups\\n\\nUse the provided backup script:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Manual backup\\n./scripts/backup_db.sh\\n\\n# Automated daily backups (add to crontab)\\ncrontab -e\\n# Add: 0 2 * * * /path/to/vehicle-speed-tracking/scripts/backup_db.sh\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Redis Configuration\\n\\nRedis is used for caching API responses, managing WebSocket connection state, and queuing video processing jobs.\\n\\n### Production Redis Configuration\\n\\nUpdate the Redis service in `docker-compose.yml`:\\n\\n```yaml\\nredis:\\n  image: redis:7-alpine\\n  command: redis-server --appendonly yes --maxmemory 512mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru\\n  volumes:\\n    - redis_data:/data\\n```\\n\\nKey settings:\\n- **appendonly yes** — Enables persistence for job queue durability\\n- **maxmemory 512mb** — Prevents Redis from consuming too much memory\\n- **maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru** — Evicts least recently used keys when memory limit is reached\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Nginx Configuration\\n\\nThe provided `nginx/nginx.conf` is pre-configured for the platform with:\\n\\n- Reverse proxy to frontend (port 3000) and backend (port 8000)\\n- WebSocket support for real-time updates (`/api/v1/ws/`)\\n- Gzip compression for static assets and API responses\\n- Client body size limit for video uploads (500MB)\\n- Security headers (X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, etc.)\\n\\n### Performance Tuning\\n\\nFor high-traffic deployments, increase worker connections:\\n\\n```nginx\\nworker_processes auto;          # One worker per CPU core\\nworker_connections 2048;        # Concurrent connections per worker\\nclient_body_buffer_size 128k;   # Buffer for uploads\\nproxy_buffer_size 128k;         # Proxy buffer size\\nproxy_buffers 4 256k;           # Number and size of proxy buffers\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Scaling Strategy\\n\\n### Horizontal Scaling\\n\\nThe platform supports horizontal scaling of the backend service:\\n\\n```yaml\\n# Scale to 3 backend instances\\ndocker compose up -d --scale backend=3\\n```\\n\\nUpdate the Nginx upstream configuration to load balance:\\n\\n```nginx\\nupstream backend {\\n    least_conn;\\n    server backend_1:8000;\\n    server backend_2:8000;\\n    server backend_3:8000;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### GPU Worker Separation\\n\\nFor deployments with limited GPU resources, separate the API servers from the AI processing workers:\\n\\n1. Run API-only backend instances without GPU access for handling HTTP requests\\n2. Run dedicated worker instances with GPU access for video processing\\n3. Use Redis as the job queue to distribute work to GPU workers\\n\\n### Database Scaling\\n\\nFor high-volume deployments:\\n- Use PostgreSQL read replicas for analytics queries\\n- Configure connection pooling with PgBouncer\\n- Consider partitioning the `detections` and `speed_logs` tables by date\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Monitoring & Logging\\n\\n### Container Health Checks\\n\\nAll services include health checks in the Docker Compose configuration. Monitor with:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Check health status of all services\\ndocker compose ps\\n\\n# View real-time logs\\ndocker compose logs -f\\n\\n# View logs for a specific service\\ndocker compose logs -f backend\\n\\n# Export logs for analysis\\ndocker compose logs --no-color > logs_$(date +%Y%m%d).txt\\n```\\n\\n### Application Metrics\\n\\nThe backend exposes health and metrics endpoints:\\n\\n- `GET /api/v1/health` — Service health check\\n- `GET /api/v1/health/detailed` — Detailed system metrics (admin only)\\n\\n### External Monitoring (Recommended)\\n\\nFor production monitoring, integrate with:\\n- **Prometheus + Grafana** — For metrics collection and visualization\\n- **Sentry** — For error tracking and alerting\\n- **Uptime Robot** — For availability monitoring\\n- **Logstash + ELK** — For centralized log management\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Backup & Recovery\\n\\n### Automated Backups\\n\\n```bash\\n# Run the backup script\\n./scripts/backup_db.sh\\n\\n# Backups are stored in ./backups/ with timestamps\\n# Example: ./backups/vstrack_db_20240115_020000.sql.gz\\n```\\n\\n### Recovery Procedure\\n\\n```bash\\n# Stop the backend to prevent writes during recovery\\ndocker compose stop backend\\n\\n# Restore the database\\ngunzip -c ./backups/vstrack_db_20240115_020000.sql.gz | \\\\\\n  docker compose exec -T db psql -U vstrack -d vstrack_db\\n\\n# Restart services\\ndocker compose start backend\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Troubleshooting\\n\\n### Common Issues\\n\\n**Backend fails to start:**\\n```bash\\n# Check logs for specific errors\\ndocker compose logs backend\\n\\n# Common causes:\\n# - Database connection refused: ensure db service is healthy\\n# - Redis connection refused: ensure redis service is healthy\\n# - Missing .env file: copy from .env.example and configure\\n```\\n\\n**Frontend build fails:**\\n```bash\\n# Check if node_modules are installed correctly\\ndocker compose exec frontend npm install\\n\\n# Clear Next.js cache\\ndocker compose exec frontend rm -rf .next\\ndocker compose restart frontend\\n```\\n\\n**WebSocket connections fail:**\\n```bash\\n# Verify Nginx WebSocket configuration\\n# Check that the Upgrade headers are set in nginx.conf\\n# Ensure the backend websocket endpoint is accessible\\ncurl -i -N -H \\"Connection: Upgrade\\" -H \\"Upgrade: websocket\\" \\\\\\n  -H \\"Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13\\" -H \\"Sec-WebSocket-Key: test\\" \\\\\\n  http://localhost/api/v1/ws/detections\\n```\\n\\n**Video processing is slow:**\\n- Use `yolov8n` (nano) model for faster processing on CPU\\n- Consider GPU acceleration with NVIDIA Container Toolkit\\n- Reduce `DETECTION_CONFIDENCE` to filter out more detections\\n- Use Haar Cascade as a lightweight alternative\\n\\n**Out of memory errors:**\\n- Increase Docker memory limit in Docker Desktop\\n- Reduce `--maxmemory` for Redis\\n- Limit concurrent video processing jobs\\n- Use smaller YOLOv8 model variants\\n\\n### Getting Help\\n\\nIf you encounter issues not covered in this guide:\\n1. Status tracking (online, offline, maintenance).\\n\\n**vehicles** — Tracked vehicle instances with unique tracking IDs, vehicle type classification, speed statistics (avg, min, max), lane assignment, and camera association.\\n\\n**detections** — Individual detection events with bounding box coordinates, confidence scores, vehicle type, and frame metadata stored as JSON.\\n\\n**speed_logs** — Timestamped speed readings for each vehicle with position data, frame numbers, and overspeed flags.\\n\\n### Migrations with Alembic\\n\\n```bash\\ncd backend\\n\\n# Create a migration after modifying models\\nalembic revision --autogenerate -m \\"add vehicle color field\\"\\n\\n# Apply all pending migrations\\nalembic upgrade head\\n\\n# Check current migration state\\nalembic current\\n\\n# Rollback the last migration\\nalembic downgrade -1\\n\\n# View migration history\\nalembic history\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Redis Setup\\n\\nRedis serves three purposes in the platform:\\n\\n1. **API tracked in Redis\\n3. **Job Queue** — Video processing jobs are enqueued in Redis for background worker consumption\\n\\n### Verifying Redis Connection\\n\\n```bash\\n# Connect to Redis {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Architecture Deep-Dive\\n\\nThis document provides an in-depth look at the architecture, design decisions, and data flow of the Vehicle Detection & Speed Tracking Platform.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## System Architecture\\n\\nThe platform follows a microservices-inspired architecture with clear separation of concerns across three primary services: the Next.js frontend, the FastAPI backend, and the AI processing engine. These services communicate through REST APIs and WebSocket connections, with PostgreSQL as the persistent data store and Redis as the caching and messaging layer.\\n\\n### Service Communication\\n\\n```\\n┌──────────┐    HTTP/REST     ┌──────────┐    SQL/Async     ┌──────────┐\\n│          │ ───────────────► │          │ ───────────────► │          │\\n│ Frontend │    WebSocket     │ Backend  │                  │PostgreSQL│\\n│  Next.js │ ◄─────────────  │ FastAPI  │ ◄─────────────── │          │\\n│          │                  │          │    Query Results  │          │\\n└──────────┘                  │          │                   └──────────┘\\n                              │          │\\n                              │          │    Pub/Sub       ┌──────────┐\\n                              │          │ ───────────────► │          │\\n                              │          │                  │  Redis   │\\n                              │          │ ◄─────────────── │          │\\n                              │          │    Cache/Jobs    │          │\\n                              └──────────┘                   └──────────┘\\n                                    │\\n                                    │ Python imports\\n                                    ▼\\n                              ┌──────────┐\\n                              │          │\\n                              │AI Engine │\\n                              │ OpenCV   │\\n                              │ YOLOv8   │\\n                              │ dlib     │\\n                              └──────────┘\\n```\\n\\n### Request Lifecycle\\n\\nA typical request to process a video follows this path:\\n\\n1. The user uploads a video through the frontend\'s drag-and-drop interface\\n2. The frontend sends a multipart/form-data POST request to `/api/v1/video/upload`\\n3. The backend validates the file, saves it to disk, and creates a processing job\\n4. The job is enqueued in Redis and a background task begins processing\\n5. The AI Engine pipeline processes each frame: detection → tracking → speed → lane\\n6. Detection events and speed logs are written to PostgreSQL\\n7. WebSocket messages are broadcast to subscribed frontend clients\\n8. The frontend dashboard updates in real-time with new data\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Backend Architecture\\n\\n### Application Structure\\n\\nThe FastAPI backend follows a layered architecture pattern:\\n\\n**API Layer** (`app/api/v1/`) — Handles HTTP request/response serialization, input validation via Pydantic models, and authentication checks. Each router is responsible for a specific domain: auth, vehicles, cameras, video processing, analytics, and reports.\\n\\n**Business Logic Layer** — Contained within the API route handlers. For more complex applications, this could be extracted into a separate service layer, but the current scope allows the logic to reside alongside the route definitions without excessive coupling.\\n\\n**Data Access Layer** (`app/db/`, `app/models/`) — SQLAlchemy ORM models define the database schema, and the async session manager provides database connections with automatic session lifecycle management.\\n\\n**AI Engine Integration** (`ai_engine/`) — The AI processing modules are imported as a Python package and invoked by the video processing background tasks.\\n\\n### Middleware Stack\\n\\nThe backend applies middleware in the following order (outermost first):\\n\\n1. **CORS Middleware** — Handles cross-origin requests from the frontend\\n2. **GZip Middleware** — Compresses response bodies larger than 1KB\\n3. **Rate Limiting Middleware** — Enforces 100 requests/minute per IP address\\n4. **Exception Handlers** — Converts unhandled exceptions to consistent JSON error responses\\n\\n### Authentication Flow\\n\\nThe authentication system uses JSON Web Tokens (JWT) with a dual-token approach:\\n\\n- **Access Token**: Short-lived (30 minutes), used for API authentication\\n- **Refresh Token**: Long-lived (7 days), used to obtain new access tokens without re-authentication\\n\\nBoth tokens are signed with HMAC-SHA256 using a shared secret. The access token contains the user ID, email, and role claims, enabling role-based access control without additional database lookups on each request.\\n\\n### WebSocket Architecture\\n\\nThe WebSocket manager supports multiple channels for different event types:\\n\\n- **detections** — Real-time vehicle detection events with bounding boxes and speed readings\\n- **alerts** — Overspeed violation alerts and system notifications\\n- **analytics** — Periodic summary statistics updates\\n\\nEach client subscribes to one or more channels upon connection. The backend broadcasts messages to all subscribers of a channel when new data becomes available. Redis pub/sub can be used to distribute WebSocket messages across multiple backend instances in a scaled deployment.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## AI Engine Architecture\\n\\n### Processing Pipeline\\n\\nThe AI Engine pipeline processes video frames through four sequential stages:\\n\\n```\\nFrame Input → Detection → Tracking → Speed Estimation → Lane Assignment → Output\\n                  │            │              │                  │\\n              Vehicle      Unique ID      Speed (km/h)       Lane Number\\n              BBox +        Position       Overspeed Flag\\n              Type +        Trail          Statistics\\n              Confidence\\n```\\n\\n### Detection Strategy\\n\\nThe detection module implements a fallback pattern:\\n\\n1. Attempt to initialize YOLOv8 with the configured model variant\\n2. If YOLOv8 is available, run inference on the frame\\n3. If YOLOv8 fails (model not found, CUDA error, etc.), fall back to Haar Cascade\\n4. If both fail, return an empty detection list with an error log\\n\\nThis ensures the system remains operational even when deep learning dependencies are unavailable, which is critical for production deployments where uptime is essential.\\n\\n### Tracking Algorithm\\n\\nThe dlib correlation tracker uses a discriminative correlation filter to track objects across frames. The tracking workflow is:\\n\\n1. For each new frame, update all existing trackers to get predicted positions\\n2. Run detection on the frame to get new bounding boxes\\n3. Compute IoU (Intersection over Union) between each tracker prediction and each detection\\n4. Match detections to trackers using a greedy assignment based on IoU scores\\n5. For matched pairs, update the tracker with the detection bounding box\\n6. For unmatched detections, create new trackers with unique IDs\\n7. For unmatched trackers, increment the missing frame counter\\n8. Remove trackers that exceed the maximum missing frame threshold (default: 30)\\n\\n### Speed Estimation Algorithm\\n\\nSpeed is estimated from pixel displacement between consecutive frames using the following formula:\\n\\n```\\nspeed_kmh = (pixel_distance / pixels_per_meter) × fps × 3.6\\n```\\n\\nWhere:\\n- `pixel_distance` is the Euclidean distance between the vehicle\'s centroid in the current and previous frames\\n- `pixels_per_meter` is the calibration factor that maps pixel distances to real-world meters\\n- `fps` is the video frame rate\\n- `3.6` converts m/s to km/h\\n\\nTo reduce noise, a configurable moving average filter is applied across the last N speed readings (default: 5). A minimum displacement threshold filters out spurious readings when vehicles are nearly stationary.\\n\\n### Lane Detection Algorithm\\n\\nLane detection uses classical computer vision techniques:\\n\\n1. Convert the frame to grayscale\\n2. Apply a region of interest (ROI) mask to focus on the road surface\\n3. Apply Gaussian blur to reduce noise\\n4. Run Canny edge detection\\n5. Apply Hough line transform to detect straight lines\\n6. Filter lines by angle (remove horizontal and near-vertical lines)\\n7. Cluster remaining lines into lane boundaries\\n8. Assign each tracked vehicle to the nearest lane based on centroid position\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Frontend Architecture\\n\\n### Next.js App Router\\n\\nThe frontend uses Next.js 14 with the App Router, which provides:\\n\\n- **File-based routing** — Each directory in `src/app/` maps to a URL path\\n- **Nested layouts** — The `(dashboard)` route group shares a common layout with the sidebar\\n- **Server Components** — Initial page loads are server-rendered for fast time-to-first-byte\\n- **Client Components** — Interactive elements use the `\\"use client\\"` directive for browser-side rendering\\n\\n### State Management\\n\\nThe frontend uses a layered state management approach:\\n\\n- **Zustand** — Client-side UI state (sidebar open/close, theme preference, monitoring state) that does not require server synchronization\\n- **TanStack Query** — Server state with automatic caching, refetching, and stale-while-revalidate patterns. All API data is managed through query hooks in `src/lib/hooks.ts`\\n- **WebSocket** — Real-time data pushed from the server (detection events, alerts) that updates both the Zustand stores and triggers TanStack Query cache invalidation\\n\\n### Component Architecture\\n\\nThe UI is built with ShadCN UI components, which are accessible, unstyled Radix primitives styled with Tailwind CSS. This provides:\\n\\n- **Accessibility** — All interactive components follow WAI-ARIA patterns\\n- **Customizability** — Components are copied into the project (not installed as packages), so they can be modified\\n- **Consistency** — The Tailwind CSS design system ensures visual consistency\\n\\n### Theme System\\n\\nThe dark/light theme is implemented using CSS custom properties (variables) defined in `globals.css`. The `next-themes` library manages theme persistence and switching. All color values reference CSS variables, enabling theme changes without JavaScript re-renders.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Database Schema\\n\\n### Entity Relationships\\n\\n```\\n┌──────────┐     1:N      ┌──────────┐\\n│  Camera  │──────────────│ Vehicle  │\\n│          │              │          │\\n│ id (PK)  │              │ id (PK)  │\\n│ name     │              │ camera_id│──────┐\\n│ rtsp_url │              │ track_id │      │\\n│ ppm      │              │ type     │      │\\n│ spd_lim  │              │ avg_spd  │      │\\n└──────────┘              │ max_spd  │      │\\n                          └──────────┘      │\\n                               │            │\\n                          1:N  │       1:N  │\\n                               ▼            ▼\\n                          ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐\\n                          │Detection │ │SpeedLog  │\\n                          │          │ │          │\\n                          │ id (PK)  │ │ id (PK)  │\\n                          │ veh_id   │ │ veh_id   │\\n                          │ bbox     │ │ speed    │\\n                          │ conf     │ │ overspd  │\\n                          │ type     │ │ position │\\n                          │ frame    │ │ frame    │\\n                          └──────────┘ └──────────┘\\n\\n┌──────────┐\\n│   User   │\\n│          │\\n│ id (PK)  │\\n│ email    │\\n│ username │\\n│ role     │\\n│ password │\\n└──────────┘\\n```\\n\\n### Indexing Strategy\\n\\nKey indexes for query performance:\\n\\n- `vehicles(camera_id, last_seen)` — Dashboard queries filtering by camera and recency\\n- `speed_logs(vehicle_id, timestamp)` — Speed history lookups ordered by time\\n- `detections(vehicle_id, frame_number)` — Detection lookups by vehicle and frame\\n- `speed_logs(is_overspeed, timestamp)` — Overspeed event filtering\\n\\n### Data Retention\\n\\nFor high-traffic deployments, consider implementing data retention policies:\\n\\n- Detections older than 30 days can be aggregated into hourly summaries and the raw records deleted\\n- Speed logs older than 90 days can be archived to cold storage\\n- Vehicle records with no recent detections can be marked as inactive\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Performance Considerations\\n\\n### Backend Performance\\n\\n- **Async I/O** — FastAPI with async SQLAlchemy and asyncpg ensures non-blocking database queries\\n- **Connection Pooling** — SQLAlchemy async engine uses a connection pool (default: 5 min, 20 max connections)\\n- **Redis Caching** — Analytics endpoints cache results for 60 seconds to reduce database load\\n- **Background Tasks** — Video processing runs in background tasks to avoid blocking API requests\\n- **GZip Compression** — Responses over 1KB are automatically compressed\\n\\n### Frontend Performance\\n\\n- **Code Splitting** — Next.js automatically splits code by route, loading only what\'s needed\\n- **Static Generation** — The landing page is statically generated at build time\\n- **Image Optimization** — Next.js Image component provides automatic optimization\\n- **Lazy Loading** — Charts and heavy components are lazy-loaded below the fold\\n- **Query Caching** — TanStack Query caches API responses with configurable stale times\\n\\n### AI Engine Performance\\n\\n- **Model Selection** — YOLOv8n processes ~80 FPS on GPU, ~12 FPS on CPU; YOLOv8x is more accurate but ~10x slower\\n- **Frame Skipping** — For high-FPS videos, process every Nth frame to reduce computational load\\n- **Resolution Scaling** — Downscale frames before detection for faster inference with minimal accuracy loss\\n- **Batch Processing** — Process multiple frames simultaneously on GPU for higher throughput\\n", "file_path": "docs/architecture.md"}'}}]}
